# Grill-with-Docs-Codex — Grill Against Your Domain, Then Get Reviewed

Two acts. Act 1 aligns intent *and* keeps your living docs honest; Act 2 has a different model attack the result.

- **Act 1** is Matt Pocock's `grill-with-docs`, used under MIT (see `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`). It interrogates you, challenges your plan against `CONTEXT.md`/ADRs, and updates them inline.
- **Act 2** is the original Codex adversarial review loop — cross-model, read-only, bounded.

You enter at two points: answering the grill, and signing off the converged plan.

---

## ACT 1 — GRILL WITH DOCS (you ↔ Claude)

<what-to-do>

Interview me relentlessly about every aspect of this plan until we reach a shared understanding. Walk down each branch of the design tree, resolving dependencies between decisions one-by-one. For each question, provide your recommended answer.

Ask the questions one at a time, waiting for feedback on each question before continuing.

If a question can be answered by exploring the codebase, explore the codebase instead.

</what-to-do>

<supporting-info>

## Domain awareness

During codebase exploration, also look for existing documentation:

### File structure

Most repos have a single context:

```
/
├── CONTEXT.md
├── docs/
│   └── adr/
│       ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md
│       └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md
└── src/
```

If a `CONTEXT-MAP.md` exists at the root, the repo has multiple contexts. The map points to where each one lives:

```
/
├── CONTEXT-MAP.md
├── docs/
│   └── adr/                          ← system-wide decisions
├── src/
│   ├── ordering/
│   │   ├── CONTEXT.md
│   │   └── docs/adr/                 ← context-specific decisions
│   └── billing/
│       ├── CONTEXT.md
│       └── docs/adr/
```

Create files lazily — only when you have something to write. If no `CONTEXT.md` exists, create one when the first term is resolved. If no `docs/adr/` exists, create it when the first ADR is needed.

## During the session

### Challenge against the glossary

When the user uses a term that conflicts with the existing language in `CONTEXT.md`, call it out immediately. "Your glossary defines 'cancellation' as X, but you seem to mean Y — which is it?"

### Sharpen fuzzy language

When the user uses vague or overloaded terms, propose a precise canonical term. "You're saying 'account' — do you mean the Customer or the User? Those are different things."

### Discuss concrete scenarios

When domain relationships are being discussed, stress-test them with specific scenarios. Invent scenarios that probe edge cases and force the user to be precise about the boundaries between concepts.

### Cross-reference with code

When the user states how something works, check whether the code agrees. If you find a contradiction, surface it: "Your code cancels entire Orders, but you just said partial cancellation is possible — which is right?"

### Update CONTEXT.md inline

When a term is resolved, update `CONTEXT.md` right there. Don't batch these up — capture them as they happen. Use the format in [CONTEXT-FORMAT.md](./CONTEXT-FORMAT.md).

`CONTEXT.md` should be totally devoid of implementation details. Do not treat `CONTEXT.md` as a spec, a scratch pad, or a repository for implementation decisions. It is a glossary and nothing else.

### Offer ADRs sparingly

Only offer to create an ADR when all three are true:

1. **Hard to reverse** — the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful
2. **Surprising without context** — a future reader will wonder "why did they do it this way?"
3. **The result of a real trade-off** — there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons

If any of the three is missing, skip the ADR. Use the format in [ADR-FORMAT.md](./ADR-FORMAT.md).

</supporting-info>

### Handoff to Act 2

When the decision tree is resolved, the glossary/ADRs are updated, and we're aligned, **write the agreed plan to `PLAN.md`** (use the canonical terms from `CONTEXT.md`), then run Act 2:

```markdown
# Plan: <task>
_Locked via grill-with-docs — by Claude + <user>. Terms per CONTEXT.md._

## Goal
<one paragraph, in the project's ubiquitous language>

## Approach
<numbered, concrete steps>

## Key decisions & tradeoffs
<the contestable choices the grill resolved — link any ADRs created>

## Risks / open questions
<anything still open>

## Out of scope
<bounds>
```

Initialize `PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md`:
```markdown
# Plan Review Log: <task>
Act 1 (grill-with-docs) complete — plan locked, CONTEXT.md/ADRs updated. MAX_ROUNDS=<n>.
```

---

## ACT 2 — REVIEW (Claude ↔ Codex)

Hand the locked plan to Codex for adversarial review. Mechanics verified end-to-end (2026-06-04).

### Prerequisites
- `codex --version` ≥ 0.130 (older CLIs error on the default `gpt-5.5` model).
- Codex authenticated (`codex login`; ChatGPT account fine). On auth/model error, surface it — don't silently retry.
- Do NOT pin `-m` (config default is used; `gpt-5.x-codex` variants 400 on ChatGPT-account auth).
- **Echo the active model before Round 1** so the user can confirm: read the `model` line from `~/.codex/config.toml` (absent = "CLI default"); state it with the resolved tunables. If the user objects, stop before burning a round.

### Tunables (args, else default)
| Var | Default | Meaning |
|-----|---------|---------|
| `MAX_ROUNDS` | `5` | Hard cap. Loop ALWAYS terminates here. |
| `PLAN_FILE` | `PLAN.md` | The plan from Act 1. |
| `LOG_FILE` | `PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md` | Append-only argument transcript. |

Invoked with e.g. `rounds=3` → use it. Echo resolved values first.

### Review prompt (each round)
> You are an adversarial reviewer for an implementation plan. Be skeptical and specific — your job is to find what breaks, not to be agreeable. Read the plan at `PLAN.md` (and `CONTEXT.md`/ADRs for the domain language) and any repo files you need (you are read-only). Identify concrete flaws: security holes, race conditions, missing edge cases, schema conflicts, domain-language mismatches, wrong assumptions, observability gaps, simpler alternatives. For each, give a one-line fix. Do NOT modify any files. End with EXACTLY one line: `VERDICT: APPROVED` or `VERDICT: REVISE`.

### Round 1 — fresh session (capture `thread_id`)
```bash
codex exec -s read-only --json -o /tmp/codex-verdict.txt "$(cat REVIEW_PROMPT)" \
  < /dev/null 2>/dev/null | grep '"type":"thread.started"'
```
Parse `thread_id` from the `thread.started` line. Critique in `/tmp/codex-verdict.txt`. No verdict file + no `thread.started` = failed run (auth/model) → stop, tell the user. `2>/dev/null` hides cosmetic MCP/auth noise. **`< /dev/null` is mandatory:** `codex exec` reads stdin *in addition to* the prompt arg, so under a non-interactive driver (Claude Code's Bash tool, CI, any non-TTY pipeline) it blocks forever waiting on stdin EOF — a silent ~0% CPU hang. The redirect gives it immediate EOF.

### Rounds 2..MAX — resume SAME session
```bash
# resume REJECTS -s. Force read-only via -c sandbox_mode, or Codex inherits
# config.toml (possibly danger-full-access) and could WRITE files. Critical
# safety line — verified 2026-06-04.
codex exec resume "$THREAD_ID" -c sandbox_mode="read-only" --json \
  -o /tmp/codex-verdict.txt \
  "I revised the plan. Re-review PLAN.md — check prior findings + flag anything new. End with VERDICT: APPROVED or VERDICT: REVISE." \
  < /dev/null 2>/dev/null >/dev/null
```
The `< /dev/null` redirect is required on the resume call too — same non-interactive stdin hang as Round 1.

**Timeout guard (both rounds):** run every `codex exec` / `codex exec resume` with a 10-minute ceiling so any future stall fails loud instead of hanging silently. Via Claude Code's Bash tool, pass `timeout: 600000` on the tool call (the default 2-minute tool timeout is too short for real reviews and would kill them mid-run). In a plain shell, prefix the command with `timeout 600` (Linux / Git Bash) or `gtimeout 600` (macOS via coreutils — stock macOS has no `timeout`). If the ceiling trips, treat it as a failed run: stop and tell the user rather than retrying blind.

### Each round
1. Read verdict file; append `## Round <n> — Codex` + critique to `LOG_FILE`.
2. Last line verdict: `APPROVED` → Resolution (converged); `REVISE` → Claude decides what's worth acting on (final arbiter), revise `PLAN_FILE`, append `### Claude's response` (what changed/rejected + why), increment.
3. round > `MAX_ROUNDS` → Resolution (deadlock).

### Resolution (you sign off)
- **APPROVED:** present final plan + 3-bullet summary of what the two acts improved + round count. Ask: implement now — Codex builds it (`/codex-build`), Claude builds it, or stop? No code during either act.
- **Deadlock (cap hit, no APPROVED):** list unresolved points + Claude's counter-position; hand to user. Don't fake convergence.
- **Act 3 (optional):** user picks Codex → invoke the `codex-build` skill with `SPEC_FILE=PLAN.md` and the same `LOG_FILE`. Roles flip: Codex writes with full access, Claude reviews the diff + runs the proof; build rounds append to the same log.

---

## Hard rules
- Act 1 precedes Act 2. `CONTEXT.md` stays a glossary only — no implementation details.
- Codex read-only EVERY round (`-s read-only` first, `-c sandbox_mode="read-only"` on resume — resume has no `-s`). Never writes.
- Loop ALWAYS terminates at `MAX_ROUNDS`. Claude is final arbiter on REVISE (reject with logged reason). Code only after sign-off. `LOG_FILE` is the deliverable.

## What NOT to do
- Don't review already-written code (`/codex:review`). Don't pin `-codex` variants on ChatGPT auth. Don't let Codex edit files. Don't skip Act 1.


ARGUMENTS: LSAT U post-question view redesign: disable/remove the AI tutoring chat (Coach panel that shows "Joshua is having trouble connecting"), replace with LSAT Lab-style layout: written explanation lives directly under the stimulus in the left column, each answer choice is a dropdown/accordion that expands to show that choice's saved explanation. Spacing must not break the stimulus column. Most explanations are already saved in the DB.

# Context Map

The repo has more than one bounded context, each with its own language. This map points to where each glossary lives.

## Contexts

- [Public surface](./src/pages/CONTEXT.md) — the unauthenticated landing + sign-in page (`Auth.tsx`): how LSAT U describes itself to a prospective student.
- [Teacher workspace](./src/pages/teacher/CONTEXT.md) — Joshua's admin operator surface (`/teacher`): assigning prepared material to students and the transcript pipeline.
- [Teacher Desk page](./src/pages/teacher/desk/CONTEXT.md) — the named visual regions of the desk page itself (the boxes on screen), nested under the teacher workspace. Names the layout so we can revise it precisely.
- [Guided Drills & Explanations](./src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md) — the practice domain: guided drills, the question bank, drill instruction cards, and per-question explanations (the Phase 2 authoring work).
- [Classroom](./src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md) — the student's own room: the Classroom frame, its sections, and the flag-versus-wrong-answer distinction.
- [Reading Comprehension](./src/features/rc/CONTEXT.md) — full-passage RC drilling: passages, sessions, attempts, reveal modes, status, and the adaptive picker.
- [Activity Centre](./src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md) — coach-led skill exercises a student is assigned, and the Read, Recall, Colour drill that runs inside one: Chunk, Detail, Coverage, The Recall, Report Card.
- [Booking](./src/components/booking/CONTEXT.md) — paying for and scheduling tutoring time: Packages, Booking Passes, the public microsite, and the Single Session (Slots, Holds, Bookers).

## Relationships

- Both surfaces describe the same product but to different audiences (prospective student vs. operator), so a word can carry different weight in each. Check the right glossary before reusing a term.
- A **flag** (Classroom) and a **wrong answer** (Wrong Answer Journal) are different things on different tables: a flag is chosen mid-question, a wrong answer is earned by missing one. Never collapse the two, and never let "flagged" drift into meaning "struggled with".
- **Set** now means one thing everywhere: the group a student assembles and drills. It used to mean a passage's position within its section in Reading Comprehension; that sense is now called **Position** (the data still carries `setNumber`). The collision was retired when the LR and RC pickers merged into one surface — a primary noun that changed meaning when you flipped the mode was the confusion the merge existed to remove. See `docs/adr/0006-rc-selection-is-question-grain.md`.

# Guided Drills & Explanations

The practice domain: how LSAT U presents drills, the questions inside them, and the teaching that wraps each one. Covers the drill surface (`src/components/drill/`, `src/pages/Drill.tsx`), the question bank (`src/lib/questionLoader.ts`, `public/data/PT*-LR.json`), and the guided-drill catalog (`src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`, `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).

## Language

**Question Explanation**:
The question-level teaching shown *after* a student answers one specific question. In the schema this is the `breakdown` (conclusion, conclusionSimple, evidence, justification, objection, prediction, crucialInsight) plus `answerChoiceExplanations` (per-choice verdict + whyCorrect/whyIncorrect) on an `LRQuestion`. One per question. Currently authored for **0 of 3,964** questions — this is the headline gap (Phase 2).
_Avoid_: explanation (ambiguous), breakdown (that's only one half), answer key, rationale.

**Drill Instruction Card**:
The drill-level teaching panel shown *before* a student starts a guided drill — teaches the pattern the drill targets ("these six questions all hide the conclusion in the first sentence..."). One per guided drill. Stored as `instruction_card: { left_md, right_md }` on a guided drill. The LSAT Lab equivalent is its "Info" card (captured verbatim in `[[LSAT-LAB-LR-INFO-CARDS]]` in the vault).
_Avoid_: info card (that's LSAT Lab's name), intro, launch card, explanation.

**Question Type**:
The LSAT task a question asks — *Main Conclusion, Flaw, Necessary Assumption, Strengthen, Paradox, Must Be True*, etc. Stored as `questionType` on a question (and `question_types[]` on a guided drill). The bank's raw values are messy (110 variants, casing dupes, 67 "Unknown") and need a canonical set (~25 types).
_Avoid_: qtype in prose (fine in code), category (that's Patterns/Process), question kind.

**Question Feature**:
A finer-grained pattern tag layered on top of the type — *First Sentence Conclusion, Explain Curious Fact, Anti-Causal, Famous Flaws, Conditional Diagramming, Missing Link / Idea*. Stored as `questionFeatures[]`. 60 clean values; this vocabulary effectively defines the guided-drill taxonomy.
_Avoid_: tag, pattern (overloaded), reasoning type (that's a separate field).

**Reasoning Type**:
The underlying logical structure — *Causation, Conditional Logic, Comparisons*. Stored as `reasoningType[]`. Distinct from Question Feature.
_Avoid_: using interchangeably with Question Feature.

**Drill Signature** (proposed):
The accurate definition of what a guided drill drills = the set of Question Types + Question Features of the *actual questions in its pool* (its qids), NOT the types/features merely mentioned in its Drill Instruction Card text.
_Avoid_: deriving a drill's targets from card prose alone (it over-captures).

**Guided Drill**:
A curated, fixed practice set of LSAT questions with a Drill Instruction Card and (target state) a deterministic ordered question list. 108 exist (`guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).
_Avoid_: drill (too generic — could mean Smart Drill), set, assignment.

**Answer Navigator** (the "bubbles"):
The strip along the bottom of the official testing frame: one small empty **oval**
per question in the queue, the question number printed *below* each oval, the
current question's number bold with a thin I-beam tick above it. Rendered by
`OfficialAnswerNavigator`. Joshua calls the ovals "bubbles"; the code calls them
ovals and the strip an answer navigator. Both names mean this one thing.
One oval stands for one **question**, never for an answer choice. This matters
because "bubble" is overloaded: on paper, the bubble you fill in IS your answer
(A-E). Here it is not. An oval is a place to go, so anything that turns it into a
verdict on your answer is out of scope for it by definition.
_Avoid_: pager (that is the RC per-question control that lives in the bar's
`pager` slot), dots, progress bar (it is navigation, not a progress readout),
and using "bubble" for an A-E answer choice.

**Oval State**:
What one oval in the Answer Navigator communicates. Today there are exactly three,
and they are orthogonal: **current** (which question you are on), **answered** (a
qid present in `attemptedQids`, meaning at least one recorded attempt), and
**jumpable** (clicking moves you there, governed by `jumpEnabled`). Notably there
is no correct/incorrect state: a section never reveals, so the strip has never had
to say whether an answer was right.
_Avoid_: "completed" or "done" (answered does not mean finished or correct),
"selected" (that is an answer choice, not an oval).

**Drill Launch**:
Everything the runner needs to be told in order to start a drill: which kind of
drill, and the name of the particular run. It is carried in the URL and nowhere
else, so any drill can be refreshed, bookmarked, or reopened. A Drill Launch
names a run; it does not contain one. The questions, the answers already given
and the position in the queue live in the Run Record.
_Avoid_: route state, config (that word currently means two different things in
the code, a filter and a fixed question list), params, drill options.

**Drill Run Spec**:
A Drill Launch after its row has been read: the mode, the question list or the
filter that draws one, and the ids saying which stored row this run belongs to.
Built by `drillRunSpecFromLaunch` and handed to the runner through context by
`DrillLaunchGate`, which is the only way the runner learns what it is running.
The Drill Launch is what a link says; the Drill Run Spec is what the runner is
told. One is written and read from a URL, the other never touches one.
_Avoid_: route state (the shape this replaced, and it read the URL a second
time), drill config (that word already means a filter or a fixed list), session.

**Run Record**:
The durable row, per student per run, holding what a run actually consists of:
the ordered question list, every attempt, the position, and the clock already
spent. Survives a closed tab, a sign-out, and a different device. It is the
reason a Drill Launch can be short: the launch names the run and the Run Record
supplies its contents. A named generated run is keyed by that name, such as a
Bootcamp day and part or a Guided drill id, so a fresh draw cannot strand the
record behind a different question list.
_Avoid_: resume state (that is what it is for, not what it is), snapshot (that is
one write to it), session (overloaded: `DrillSession` is the in-memory object for
the current screen, and an auth session is a third thing).

import * as React from "react";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import type { LrExplanationData, LrStyleKey } from "@/hooks/useLrExplanation";

/**
 * The drawer's Explanation tab: everything about the question as a whole.
 * Per-choice content lives inline under the choices themselves
 * (`InlineChoiceExplanation`), not here.
 *
 * Two rules survive from the retired `ExplanationPanel`:
 *  - The style selector renders only when the source published more than one
 *    style for this question (~5% of the bank).
 *  - Where a non-standard style carries no steps, the source said "(same as
 *    Standard)" and did not repeat the text. Rendering nothing would
 *    misrepresent that, so Standard's breakdown is borrowed and labelled. The
 *    absence only ever means "identical to Standard", never "none exists".
 */

export interface DrillExplanationContext {
  data?: LrExplanationData | null;
  isLoading?: boolean;
  isError?: boolean;
  /** Nothing is shown until the answer is revealed. */
  revealed: boolean;
  /** Shared with the inline layer, so switching style swaps both surfaces. */
  activeStyle: LrStyleKey | null;
  onStyleChange: (style: LrStyleKey) => void;
  /** Hand-off to the AI tutor when the source published no prose. */
  onAskTutor?: () => void;
}

export default function DrillExplanationTab({
  data,
  isLoading,
  isError,
  revealed,
  activeStyle,
  onStyleChange,
  onAskTutor,
}: DrillExplanationContext) {
  const styles = data?.styles ?? [];
  const current = styles.find((s) => s.style === activeStyle) ?? styles[0];

  const standardSteps = styles.find((s) => s.style === "standard")?.steps ?? [];
  const borrowsStandard =
    current != null &&
    current.style !== "standard" &&
    current.steps.length === 0 &&
    standardSteps.length > 0;
  const steps = borrowsStandard ? standardSteps : current?.steps ?? [];

  if (!revealed) {
    return (
      <Body>
        <p className="text-[13px] text-slate-400">
          The explanation unlocks once you have answered or revealed this question.
        </p>
      </Body>
    );
  }

  if (isLoading) {
    return (
      <Body>
        <p className="text-[13px] text-slate-400">Loading explanation…</p>
      </Body>
    );
  }

  if (isError) {
    return (
      <Body>
        <p className="text-[13px] text-slate-400">
          Could not load the explanation. Try again in a moment.
        </p>
      </Body>
    );
  }

  if (!data?.hasAuthored) {
    return (
      <Body>
        <p className="text-[13px] leading-relaxed text-slate-500">
          No written explanation was published for this question.
        </p>
        {onAskTutor && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={onAskTutor}
            className="self-start rounded-full border border-slate-400 bg-white px-4 py-1.5 text-[13px] font-medium text-slate-700 transition-colors hover:bg-slate-100"
          >
            Ask Joshua about this question
          </button>
        )}
      </Body>
    );
  }

  return (
    <Body>
      {data.correctChoice && (
        <p className="text-[13px] text-slate-600">
          Correct answer:{" "}
          <span className="font-semibold text-slate-900">{data.correctChoice}</span>
        </p>
      )}

      {styles.length > 1 && (
        <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-1.5">
          {styles.map((s) => {
            const active = current != null && s.style === current.style;
            return (
              <button
                key={s.style}
                type="button"
                onClick={() => onStyleChange(s.style)}
                aria-pressed={active}
                className={cn(
                  "rounded-full px-3 py-1 text-[12px] font-semibold transition-colors",
                  active
                    ? "bg-slate-900 text-white"
                    : "border border-slate-300 bg-white text-slate-600 hover:bg-slate-100",
                )}
              >
                {s.label}
              </button>
            );
          })}
        </div>
      )}

      {steps.length > 0 && (
        <div className="flex flex-col gap-2.5 border-t border-slate-100 pt-4">
          <h3 className="text-[11px] uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-400">
            Argument breakdown
            {borrowsStandard && (
              <span className="ml-2 normal-case tracking-normal text-slate-400">
                same as Standard
              </span>
            )}
          </h3>
          {steps.map((step) => (
            <div key={step.position} className="text-[13px] leading-relaxed">
              <span className="font-semibold text-slate-900">{step.label}: </span>
              <span className="whitespace-pre-line text-slate-600">{step.body}</span>
            </div>
          ))}
        </div>
      )}

      <p className="border-t border-slate-100 pt-4 text-[12px] italic leading-relaxed text-slate-400">
        Why each choice is right or wrong sits under that choice, beside the question.
      </p>
    </Body>
  );
}

function Body({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-1 flex-col gap-4 overflow-y-auto border-t border-slate-100 px-5 py-4">
      {children}
    </div>
  );
}
import * as React from "react";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * The per-choice explanation layer that sits directly beneath an answer choice
 * in `OfficialQuestionPane`. Replaces the old dark `ExplanationPanel` sheet.
 *
 * The explanation is ALWAYS VISIBLE. It used to sit behind a "Why this is
 * wrong" disclosure, which cost a click per choice and, on a shared screen
 * during teaching, hid the very thing being taught. Reading five choices meant
 * five clicks. The prose is the point of the review screen, so it is set at
 * reading size and shown outright.
 *
 * Two rules carried over from that panel, and tightened to apply per choice
 * rather than per question:
 *  - Never invent content. A missing tag, percentage, or note renders as
 *    absent. A choice with no note gets no disclosure row at all — not an
 *    empty one, not a placeholder.
 *  - The source's nuance lives in the tag chip verbatim ("Too Weak", "Weakens,
 *    if anything"). The disclosure label only says which way the choice went.
 */

export interface InlineChoiceNote {
  /** Wrong-answer type from the source, e.g. "Out of Scope". */
  tag?: string | null;
  /** Percent of students who picked this choice. */
  studentPercent?: number | null;
  /** The choice's explanation prose. Absent for letters the source skipped. */
  note?: string | null;
}

/** Per-letter inline content for one question. A letter may legitimately be absent. */
export type InlineChoiceNotes = Record<string, InlineChoiceNote>;

interface Props {
  letter: string;
  isCorrect: boolean;
  /** The student's own pick, marked so they can find it fast. */
  isSelected: boolean;
  content: InlineChoiceNote;
}

/** True when there is nothing real to render for this letter. */
export function isInlineChoiceEmpty(content: InlineChoiceNote | undefined, isSelected: boolean) {
  if (isSelected) return false;
  if (!content) return true;
  return !content.tag && content.studentPercent == null && !content.note;
}

export default function InlineChoiceExplanation({
  letter,
  isCorrect,
  isSelected,
  content,
}: Props) {
  const { tag, studentPercent, note } = content;
  const hasNote = typeof note === "string" && note.trim().length > 0;

  if (isInlineChoiceEmpty(content, isSelected)) return null;

  return (
    <div className="mt-2.5">
      {/* Tag, percentage, and the student's own pick. Any of these may be absent. */}
      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 text-[12px]">
        {tag && (
          <span
            className={cn(
              "rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold",
              isCorrect ? "bg-green-600 text-white" : "bg-slate-200 text-slate-700",
            )}
          >
            {tag}
          </span>
        )}
        {studentPercent != null && (
          <span className="text-slate-500 tabular-nums">{studentPercent}% of students</span>
        )}
        {isSelected && (
          <span className="rounded-full border border-slate-300 px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-500">
            Your answer
          </span>
        )}
      </div>

      {/* No note means nothing here. The absence is the honest render. */}
      {hasNote && (
        <div
          className={cn(
            // A left rule in the choice's own colour anchors the prose to the
            // choice it belongs to. Without it, five explanations down a page
            // read as one undifferentiated column of text.
            "mt-2 border-l-2 pl-3",
            isCorrect ? "border-green-600/50" : "border-slate-300",
          )}
        >
          <p
            className={cn(
              "text-[12px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide",
              isCorrect ? "text-green-800" : "text-slate-500",
            )}
          >
            {isCorrect ? "Why this is correct" : "Why this is wrong"}
          </p>
          {/* Reading size, not caption size: this is the sentence the student
              is meant to actually read, and it carries the teaching. */}
          <p className="mt-1 whitespace-pre-line text-[15px] leading-7 text-slate-800">
            {note}
          </p>
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

import * as React from "react";
import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';

}

  eliminatedAnswers: string[];
  timeMs: number | null;
  flagged: boolean;
  answerRevealed: boolean;
}

type PanelTab = 'coach' | 'breakdown' | 'notes';

function errorMessage(error: unknown, fallback: string) {
  return error instanceof Error ? error.message : fallback;
}

interface TutorChatModalProps {
  open: boolean;
  question: LRQuestion | null;
  userAnswer: string;
  onClose: () => void;
  mode?: TutorMode;
  sessionId?: string;
  tutorContext?: TutorContext;
  onNext?: () => void;
  /** Which tab to open on. Defaults from `mode` (preview → breakdown). */
  initialTab?: PanelTab;
}

// The live coaching mode for the Coach tab, derived from drill state.
function coachModeFor(userAnswer: string, answerRevealed: boolean): TutorMode {
  if (answerRevealed) return 'post-answer';

}

  // and nothing load-bearing may depend on it. A refreshed leg used to lose

  const ptRun = state.ptRun;
  // Routing and score-UI suppression only. Never a clock or integrity decision.
  const inSitting = isSittingReturn(drillReturnTo);
  /**
   * Answer and Explanation are not rendered at all for this run.
   *
   * Two runs are sealed. A practice-test sitting, because a sitting reveals
   * nothing until it is scored and the old per-question gate quietly broke
   * that: attempt a question inside a sitting and the reveal unlocked. And a
   * run launched `?sealed=1`, which is how an assigned section is handed over.
   *
   * The full-section gate further down is a different and weaker thing. It
   * withholds the reveal until the question is attempted and then allows it.

import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';

/**
 * Authored LSAT Lab explanations, read from the `lr_*` tables.
 *
 * These are transcribed verbatim from the source and verified against LSAT
 * Lab's own API (see `ai-os/wiki/explanations/COVERAGE-AUDIT.md`). Nothing here
 * is generated: if a field is null the source did not provide it, and the UI
 * must render that absence honestly rather than filling it in.
 *
 * Coverage is deliberately partial. Roughly 350 of the ~3,770 ingested
 * questions have no authored prose at all — LSAT Lab published only a video
 * for them. `hasAuthored` is the flag callers should branch on; that is the
 * signal for falling back to the AI tutor.
 */

export type LrStyleKey = 'single' | 'standard' | 'brief' | 'another_way';

export const LR_STYLE_LABELS: Record<LrStyleKey, string> = {
  single: 'Explanation',
  standard: 'Standard',
  brief: 'Brief',
  another_way: 'Another Way',
};

/** Display order for the style selector; mirrors LSAT Lab's own ordering. */
const STYLE_ORDER: LrStyleKey[] = ['standard', 'brief', 'another_way', 'single'];

export interface LrChoice {
  letter: string;
  text: string;
  /** Wrong-answer type, e.g. "Out of Scope". Null when the source had none. */
  tag: string | null;
  /** Percent of students who picked this choice. Null when not published. */
  studentPercent: number | null;
  isCorrect: boolean;
}

export interface LrStep {
  position: number;
  label: string;
  body: string;
}

export interface LrStyle {
  style: LrStyleKey;
  label: string;
  /** Ordered argument-breakdown steps. Empty when the source had none. */
  steps: LrStep[];
  /** Per-choice prose, keyed by letter. A letter may legitimately be absent. */
  notesByLetter: Record<string, string>;
}

export interface LrExplanationData {
  questionId: string;
  preptest: number;
  section: number;
  questionNumber: number;
  questionType: string;
  correctChoice: string | null;
  choices: LrChoice[];
  styles: LrStyle[];
  /**
   * True when at least one style carries real prose — a breakdown step or a
   * per-choice note. Tags and percentages alone do not count: a question can
   * have every choice tagged and still have no authored explanation.
   */
  hasAuthored: boolean;
}

export interface LrExplanationQuery {
  pt?: number | null;
  section?: number | null;
  questionNumber?: number | null;
}

function toStyleKey(value: string): LrStyleKey {
  return (STYLE_ORDER as string[]).includes(value) ? (value as LrStyleKey) : 'single';
}

import * as React from "react";
import { DrillSplit } from '@/components/drill/DrillSplit';
import { Check, Eye, EyeOff, Flag, X } from "lucide-react";
import { HighlightedText } from "@/components/drill/HighlightedText";
import InlineChoiceExplanation, {
  type InlineChoiceNotes,
} from "@/components/drill/official/InlineChoiceExplanation";
import type { Highlight } from "@/lib/highlightUtils";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

interface Question {
  qid: string;
  qnum?: number | string;
  stimulus: string;
  questionStem: string;
  answerChoices: Record<string, string>;
}

interface Props {
  question: Question;
  displayNumber: number;
  selectedAnswer: string;
  eliminatedAnswers: Set<string>;
  isFlagged: boolean;
  answerRevealed?: boolean;
  correctAnswer?: string;
  /**
   * Per-choice explanation content, rendered inline beneath each choice once
   * the answer is revealed. Omit it entirely (section mode, or before reveal)
   * and no inline layer renders at all.
   */
  inlineNotes?: InlineChoiceNotes | null;
  highlights: Highlight[];
  highlightMode: "none" | "yellow" | "pink" | "orange" | "underline" | "erase";
  searchTerm?: string;
  fontScale?: number;
  onAnswerSelect: (key: string) => void;
  onEliminate: (key: string) => void;
  onToggleFlag: () => void;
  onTextSelection: (e: React.MouseEvent<HTMLDivElement>, section: "stimulus" | "stem") => void;
  onHighlightClick: (highlightId: string) => void;
  /**
   * Rendered between the stem and the choices. Blind review puts its Actual/BR
   * tabs here so it can run inside this exact pane rather than reimplementing
   * the exam screen a second time in a different visual language.
   */
  aboveChoices?: React.ReactNode;
}

function stripDataArtifacts(text: string): string {
  return text
    .replace(/\s*\{#\d+\}\s*/g, " ")
    .replace(/[ \t]{2,}/g, " ")
    .replace(/\n[ \t]+/g, "\n")
    .trim();
}

function cleanAnswerChoice(key: string, text: string): string {
  const escapedKey = key.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, "\\$&");
  return stripDataArtifacts(text)
    .replace(new RegExp(`^\\s*[-–—]?\\s*\\[${escapedKey}\\]\\s*`, "i"), "")
    .replace(new RegExp(`^\\s*[-–—]?\\s*\\(${escapedKey}\\)\\s*`, "i"), "")
    .replace(new RegExp(`^\\s*[-–—]?\\s*${escapedKey}[.)]\\s*`, "i"), "")
    .trim();
}

export default function OfficialQuestionPane({
  question,
  displayNumber,
  selectedAnswer,
  eliminatedAnswers,
  isFlagged,
  answerRevealed = false,
  correctAnswer,
  inlineNotes,
  highlights,
  highlightMode,
  searchTerm,
  fontScale = 1,
  onAnswerSelect,
  onEliminate,
  onToggleFlag,
  onTextSelection,
  onHighlightClick,
  aboveChoices,
}: Props) {
  const stimulusHighlights = highlights.filter((h) => h.section === "stimulus");
  const stemHighlights = highlights.filter((h) => h.section === "stem");
  const stimulusText = stripDataArtifacts(question.stimulus);
  const stemText = stripDataArtifacts(question.questionStem);
  const answerEntries = Object.entries(question.answerChoices).map(([key, text]) => [
    key,
    cleanAnswerChoice(key, text),
  ] as const);

  // 7Sage sets both the stimulus and the answer choices at 19px and the
  // student reads ours next to theirs, so the two match rather than land a
  // couple of pixels apart. The stimulus carries the extra weight of the serif
  // face; stem and choices stay on the sans stack at the same size.
  const baseSize = 19 * fontScale;
  const officialContentTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    color: "#111827",
    fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif',
    fontSize: `${baseSize}px`,
    fontWeight: 400,
    lineHeight: 1.6,
  };
  const stimStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    ...officialContentTextStyle,
    fontFamily: "Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif",
    fontSize: `${19 * fontScale}px`,
    lineHeight: 1.7,
    // Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. Left on `auto` the
    // browser picks the cut from the rendered px size, which drifts as the
    // student changes fontScale. Pin it to the text-optimised end so hairlines
    // stay the same weight at every scale.
    fontOpticalSizing: "none",
    fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
  };
  const stemStyle: React.CSSProperties = officialContentTextStyle;

              // Cap the reading measure. The frame is max-w-[1400px] and this column

              "[&_p]:mb-4 [&_p:last-child]:mb-0",
              "bg-white",
            )}
            style={stimStyle}
            onMouseUp={(e) => onTextSelection(e, "stimulus")}
          >
            <HighlightedText
              text={stimulusText}
              highlights={stimulusHighlights}
              onHighlightClick={onHighlightClick}
              eraserMode={highlightMode === "erase"}
              searchTerm={searchTerm}
            />
          </div>
        }
        right={
          // Question column
          <div className="min-w-0 px-6 py-7 sm:pl-10 sm:pr-8 bg-white overflow-y-auto" style={stemStyle}>
            <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-3">
              <div
                className="flex-1 flex items-start gap-2"
                onMouseUp={(e) => onTextSelection(e, "stem")}
              >
                <span className="font-bold tabular-nums shrink-0 min-w-[20px] text-slate-950">
                  {displayNumber}.
                </span>
                <div className="flex-1 text-slate-950">
                  <HighlightedText
                    text={stemText}
                    highlights={stemHighlights}
                    onHighlightClick={onHighlightClick}
                    eraserMode={highlightMode === "erase"}
                    searchTerm={searchTerm}
                  />
                </div>
              </div>
              <button
                type="button"
                onClick={onToggleFlag}
                aria-pressed={isFlagged}
                aria-label={isFlagged ? "Remove flag" : "Flag question"}
                title={isFlagged ? "Remove flag" : "Flag question"}
                className={cn(
                  "shrink-0 p-1.5 rounded transition-colors",
                  isFlagged ? "bg-amber-100" : "hover:bg-slate-100",
                )}
              >
                <Flag
                  className={cn(
                    "w-[24px] h-[24px] transition-colors text-slate-950",
                    isFlagged && "fill-amber-400",
                  )}
                  strokeWidth={2}
                />

            </div>

            <ul className="mt-4 space-y-3.5">
              {answerEntries.map(([key, text]) => {
                const isSelected = selectedAnswer === key;
                const isEliminated = eliminatedAnswers.has(key);
                const isCorrect = answerRevealed && correctAnswer === key;
                // The choice they committed to, once it is known to be wrong.
                // Without this a wrong answer stayed the same neutral grey as an
                // unrevealed selection, so revealing said what was right without
                // ever saying what they did.
                const isWrongPick = answerRevealed && isSelected && correctAnswer !== key;
                // The inline layer only ever appears once the answer is revealed.
                const inline =
                  answerRevealed && inlineNotes ? inlineNotes[key] ?? {} : undefined;

                return (

                    key={key}
                    className={cn(
                      "group relative rounded-lg border transition-all duration-200",
                      "grid grid-cols-[auto_1fr_auto] items-start gap-3 p-3",
                      isCorrect
                        ? "border-green-500 bg-green-50"
                        : isWrongPick
                        ? "border-red-500 bg-red-50"
                        : isSelected
                        ? "border-slate-500 bg-slate-100 shadow-sm"
                        : isEliminated
                        ? "border-transparent bg-white opacity-55 grayscale-[0.5]"
                        : "border-transparent bg-white hover:border-slate-200 hover:bg-slate-50",
                    )}
                  >
                    <button
                      type="button"
                      onClick={() => !isEliminated && !answerRevealed && onAnswerSelect(key)}
                      disabled={isEliminated || answerRevealed}
                      className={cn(
                        "mt-0.5 flex h-6 w-6 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded border font-semibold transition-all",
                        isCorrect
                          ? "border-green-600 bg-green-500 text-white"
                          : isWrongPick
                          ? "border-red-600 bg-red-500 text-white"
                          : isSelected
                          ? "border-slate-900 bg-slate-900 text-white"
                          : "border-transparent text-slate-950 group-hover:border-slate-300",
                      )}
                      style={choiceBadgeStyle}
                    >
                      {key}
                    </button>
                    <button
                      type="button"
                      onClick={() => !isEliminated && !answerRevealed && onAnswerSelect(key)}
                      disabled={isEliminated || answerRevealed}
                      className={cn(
                        "text-left leading-relaxed transition-colors",
                        isCorrect
                          ? "text-green-900"
                          : isWrongPick
                          ? "text-red-900"
                          : isSelected
                          ? "text-slate-950"
                          : "text-slate-950 group-hover:text-slate-950",
                      )}
                      style={choiceTextStyle}
                    >
                      {text}
                    </button>
                    {answerRevealed && (isCorrect || isWrongPick) ? (
                      // Colour alone must never carry the verdict (ADR 0001), and
                      // once the answer is out the cross-out control is dead
                      // anyway, so its corner becomes the icon and the label.
                      <span
                        role="img"
                        aria-label={isCorrect ? "Correct answer" : "Your answer, incorrect"}
                        title={isCorrect ? "Correct answer" : "Your answer, incorrect"}
                        className={cn(
                          "mt-0.5 flex h-6 w-6 shrink-0 items-center justify-center",
                          isCorrect ? "text-green-700" : "text-red-700",
                        )}
                      >
                        {isCorrect ? <Check size={18} /> : <X size={18} />}
                      </span>
                    ) : (
                    <button
                      type="button"
                      onClick={(event) => {
                        event.stopPropagation();
                        if (!answerRevealed) onEliminate(key);
                      }}
                      disabled={answerRevealed}
                      // A screen reader announced this as an unlabelled button.
                      // RC's equivalent has carried both of these since it was
                      // written; this is LR catching up to it.
                      aria-label={
                        isEliminated ? `Restore choice ${key}` : `Cross out choice ${key}`
                      }
                      title={isEliminated ? "Restore" : "Cross out"}
                      className={cn(
                        "mt-0.5 p-1 transition-opacity",
                        isEliminated ? "text-slate-700" : "text-slate-400 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 hover:text-slate-800",
                      )}
                    >
                      {isEliminated ? <EyeOff size={16} /> : <Eye size={16} />}
                    </button>
                    )}
                    {inline && (
                      <div className="col-start-2 col-end-4">
                        <InlineChoiceExplanation
                          letter={key}
                          isCorrect={isCorrect}
                          isSelected={isSelected}
                          content={inline}
                        />
                      </div>
                    )}
                  </li>
                );
              })}
            </ul>
          </div>
        }
      />
    </div>
  );
}

name: watch
description: Watch a video (URL or local path). Downloads with yt-dlp, extracts auto-scaled frames with ffmpeg, pulls the transcript from captions (or Whisper API fallback), and hands the result to Claude so it can answer questions about what's in the video.

# /watch — Claude watches a video

You don't have a video input; this skill gives you one. A Python script downloads the video, extracts frames as JPEGs, gets a timestamped transcript (native captions first, then Whisper API as fallback), and prints frame paths. You then `Read` each frame path to see the images and combine them with the transcript to answer the user.

## Step 0 — Setup preflight (runs every `/watch` invocation, silent on success)

**Python interpreter:** every `python3 ...` command in this skill is for macOS/Linux. On **Windows**, substitute `python` — the `python3` command on Windows is the Microsoft Store stub and will not run the script.

Before every `/watch` run, verify that dependencies and an API key are in place:

```bash
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py" --check
```

This is a <100ms lookup. On exit 0, the script emits **nothing** — proceed to Step 1 without comment. **Do NOT announce "setup is complete" to the user** — they don't need a status message on every turn. The only acceptable user-visible output from Step 0 is when remediation is required.

On non-zero exit, follow the table:

| Exit | Meaning | Action |
|------|---------|--------|
| `2` | Missing binaries (`ffmpeg` / `ffprobe` / `yt-dlp`) | Run installer |
| `3` | No Whisper API key | Run installer to scaffold `.env`, then ask user for a key |
| `4` | Both missing | Run installer, then ask for a key |

The installer is idempotent — safe to re-run:

```bash
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py"
```

On macOS with Homebrew, it auto-installs `ffmpeg` and `yt-dlp`. On Linux/Windows, it prints the exact install commands for the user to run. It scaffolds `~/.config/watch/.env` with commented placeholders at `0600` perms, and writes `SETUP_COMPLETE=true` once deps + a key are in place so the next session knows this user has already been through the wizard.

**If an API key is still missing after install:** use `AskUserQuestion` to ask the user whether they have a Groq API key (preferred — cheaper, faster) or an OpenAI key. Then write it into `~/.config/watch/.env` — set the matching `GROQ_API_KEY=...` or `OPENAI_API_KEY=...` line. If they don't want to set up Whisper, proceed with `--no-whisper` and tell them videos without native captions will come back frames-only.

**Structured mode (optional):** `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py" --json` emits `{status, first_run, missing_binaries, whisper_backend, has_api_key, config_file, platform}` where `status` is one of `ready | needs_install | needs_key | needs_install_and_key`. Use this when you need to branch on specifics (e.g. "is this the user's very first run?" → `first_run: true`).

Within a single session, you can skip Step 0 on follow-up `/watch` calls — once `--check` returned 0, nothing about the environment changes between turns.

## When to use

- User pastes a video URL (YouTube, Vimeo, X, TikTok, Twitch clip, most yt-dlp-supported sites) and asks about it.
- User points at a local video file (`.mp4`, `.mov`, `.mkv`, `.webm`, etc.) and asks about it.
- User types `/watch <url-or-path> [question]`.

## Recommended limits

- **Best accuracy: videos under 10 minutes.** Frame coverage scales inversely with duration.
- **Hard caps: 100 frames total and 2 fps.** Token cost grows with frame count, so the script targets a frame budget by duration (and never exceeds 2 fps even when the budget would imply more):
  - ≤30s → ~1-2 fps (up to 30 frames)
  - 30s-1min → ~40 frames
  - 1-3min → ~60 frames
  - 3-10min → ~80 frames
  - \>10min → 100 frames, sparsely spaced (warning printed)
- If the user hands you a long video, consider asking whether they want a specific section before burning tokens on a sparse scan.

## How to invoke

**Step 1 — parse the user input.** Separate the video source (URL or path) from any question the user asked. Example: `/watch https://youtu.be/abc what language is this in?` → source = `https://youtu.be/abc`, question = `what language is this in?`.

**Step 2 — run the watch script.** Pass the source verbatim. Do not shell-escape it yourself beyond normal quoting:

```bash
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" "<source>"
```

Optional flags:
- `--start T` / `--end T` — focus on a section. Accepts `SS`, `MM:SS`, or `HH:MM:SS`. When either is set, fps auto-scales denser (see "Focusing on a section" below).
- `--max-frames N` — lower the cap for tighter token budget (e.g. `--max-frames 40`)
- `--resolution W` — change frame width in px (default 512; bump to 1024 only if the user needs to read on-screen text)
- `--fps F` — override auto-fps (clamped to 2 fps max)
- `--out-dir DIR` — keep working files somewhere specific (default: an auto-generated tmp dir)
- `--whisper groq|openai` — force a specific Whisper backend (default: prefer Groq if both keys exist)
- `--no-whisper` — disable the Whisper fallback entirely (frames-only if no captions)

### Focusing on a section (higher frame rate)

When the user asks about a specific moment — "what happens at the 2 minute mark?", "zoom into 0:45 to 1:00", "the first 10 seconds" — pass `--start` and/or `--end`. The script switches to focused-mode budgets, which are denser than full-video budgets (still capped at 2 fps):

- ≤5s → 2 fps (up to 10 frames)
- 5-15s → 2 fps (up to 30 frames)
- 15-30s → ~2 fps (up to 60 frames)
- 30-60s → ~1.3 fps (up to 80 frames)
- 60-180s → ~0.6 fps (100 frames, capped)

Focused mode is the right call for:
- Any moment/range the user names explicitly ("around 2:30", "the intro", "the last 30 seconds").
- Any video longer than ~10 minutes where the user's question is about a specific part — running focused on the relevant section is far more useful than a sparse scan of the whole thing.
- Re-runs after a full scan didn't have enough detail in some region.

Transcript is auto-filtered to the same range. Frame timestamps are absolute (real video timeline, not offset-from-start).

Examples:
```bash
# Last 10 seconds of a 1 minute video
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" video.mp4 --start 50 --end 60

# Zoom into 2:15 → 2:45 at 3 fps (90 frames)
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" "$URL" --start 2:15 --end 2:45 --fps 3

# From 1h12m to the end of the video
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" "$URL" --start 1:12:00
```

**Step 3 — Read every frame path the script lists.** The Read tool renders JPEGs directly as images for you. Read all frames in a single message (parallel tool calls) so you see them together. The frames are in chronological order with a `t=MM:SS` timestamp so you can align them to the transcript.

**Step 4 — answer the user.** You now have two streams of evidence:
- **Frames** — what's on screen at each timestamp
- **Transcript** — what's said at each timestamp. The report's header shows the source (`captions` = yt-dlp pulled native subs; `whisper (groq)` or `whisper (openai)` = transcribed by API).

If the user asked a specific question, answer it directly citing timestamps. If they didn't ask anything, summarize what happens in the video — structure, key moments, notable visuals, spoken content.

**Step 5 — clean up.** The script prints a working directory at the end. If the user isn't going to ask follow-ups about this video, delete it with `rm -rf <dir>`. If they might, leave it in place.

## Transcription

The script gets a timestamped transcript in one of two ways:

1. **Native captions (free, preferred).** yt-dlp pulls manual or auto-generated subtitles from the source platform if available.
2. **Whisper API fallback.** If no captions came back (or the source is a local file), the script extracts audio (`ffmpeg -vn -ac 1 -ar 16000 -b:a 64k`, ~0.5 MB/min) and uploads it to whichever Whisper API has a key configured:
   - **Groq** — `whisper-large-v3`. Preferred default: cheaper, faster. Get a key at console.groq.com/keys.
   - **OpenAI** — `whisper-1`. Fallback. Get a key at platform.openai.com/api-keys.

Both keys live in `~/.config/watch/.env`. The script prefers Groq when both are set; override with `--whisper openai` to force OpenAI. Use `--no-whisper` to skip the fallback entirely.

## Failure modes and handling

- **Setup preflight failed** → run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py"` (auto-installs ffmpeg/yt-dlp via brew on macOS, scaffolds the `.env`). For API key, ask the user via `AskUserQuestion` and write it to `~/.config/watch/.env`.
- **No transcript available** → captions missing AND (no Whisper key OR Whisper API failed). Script prints a hint pointing to setup. Proceed frames-only and tell the user.
- **Long video warning printed** → acknowledge it in your answer. Offer to re-run focused on a specific section via `--start`/`--end` rather than a sparse full-video scan.
- **Download fails** → yt-dlp's error goes to stderr. If it's a login-required or region-locked video, tell the user plainly; do not keep retrying.
- **Whisper request fails** → the error is printed to stderr (likely: invalid key, rate limit, or 25 MB upload limit on a very long video). The report will say "none available" for transcript. You can retry with `--whisper openai` if Groq failed (or vice versa).

## Token efficiency

This skill burns tokens primarily on frames. Order of magnitude:
- 80 frames at 512px wide is roughly 50-80k image tokens depending on aspect ratio.
- The transcript is cheap (a few thousand tokens at most for a 10-minute video).
- Bumping `--resolution` to 1024 roughly quadruples the image tokens per frame. Only do it when necessary.

If you already watched a video this session and the user asks a follow-up, do **not** re-run the script — you already have the frames and transcript in context. Just answer from what you have.

## Security & Permissions

**What this skill does:**
- Runs `yt-dlp` locally to download the video and pull native captions when the source supports them (public data; the request goes directly to whatever host the URL points at)
- Runs `ffmpeg` / `ffprobe` locally to extract frames as JPEGs and, when Whisper is needed, a mono 16 kHz audio clip
- Sends the extracted audio clip to Groq's Whisper API (`api.groq.com/openai/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `GROQ_API_KEY` is set (preferred — cheaper, faster)
- Sends the extracted audio clip to OpenAI's audio transcription API (`api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set and Groq is not, or when `--whisper openai` is forced
- Writes the downloaded video, frames, audio, and an intermediate transcript to a working directory under the system temp dir (or `--out-dir` if specified) so Claude can `Read` them
- Reads / creates `~/.config/watch/.env` (mode `0600`) to store the Whisper API key(s) and a `SETUP_COMPLETE` marker. As a fallback, also reads `.env` in the current working directory

**What this skill does NOT do:**
- Does not upload the video itself to any API — only the extracted audio goes out, and only when native captions are missing AND Whisper is not disabled with `--no-whisper`
- Does not access any platform account (no login, no session cookies, no posting)
- Does not share API keys between providers (Groq key only goes to `api.groq.com`, OpenAI key only goes to `api.openai.com`)
- Does not log, cache, or write API keys to stdout, stderr, or output files
- Does not persist anything outside the working directory and `~/.config/watch/.env` — clean up the working directory when you're done (Step 5)

**Bundled scripts:** `scripts/watch.py` (entry point), `scripts/download.py` (yt-dlp wrapper), `scripts/frames.py` (ffmpeg frame extraction), `scripts/transcribe.py` (caption selection + Whisper orchestration), `scripts/whisper.py` (Groq / OpenAI clients), `scripts/setup.py` (preflight + installer)

Review scripts before first use to verify behavior.

## assistant

Watched it (32 s, frames only, no audio needed). What the recording shows, in order:

1. **0:00** Explanation is on: breakdown under the stimulus (Background / Opposing Idea / Evidence / Conclusion / Evaluate), all five choices expanded with tag, note, "% of students", chevron on each. Tooltip on the pill reads "Hide explanations".
2. **0:03** Click Explanation → everything folds away. Plain question, five grey choice rows, no colour.
3. **0:08** Click Answer → A's letter badge turns green. Nothing else changes: no notes, no red, choices still plain.
4. **0:12** Click Answer again (tooltip "Show correct answer") → green gone.
5. **0:17** Hover "Reset Response" → not-allowed cursor, because no response is selected. A separate "Hide Answers" button sits under the choices.

So the three controls are independent toggles, none of them lock anything, and the choice dropdowns open expanded with a per-choice chevron. That answers Question 4: expanded by default, LSAT Lab style.

Most of this already exists in LSAT U (`toggleAnswerReveal`, `toggleExplanation`). What's genuinely new: the wrong-answer path stops forcing a lock, the breakdown moves under the stimulus, the chevrons, and **Reset Response**.

**Last question: what does Reset Response do to the attempt?**

Recommendation: the first submit is logged and stays logged (score, journal, history all keep it). Reset clears the highlighted pick and the red/green so the question reads untouched and she can pick again; a second submit is *not* logged as a new attempt, it's practice on a question she already answered. Reset is greyed out until she has picked something, exactly like the video.

Agree, or should a re-submit after Reset count as a fresh attempt?

6836bdb RRC demos: Round 8 films, text written in the air (#122)
// "preview" is the pre-submission structural breakdown (Conclusion / Evidence /
// Evaluate / Goal), answer-blind. The other three are the live coaching modes.
export type TutorMode = "preview" | "pre-answer" | "socratic" | "post-answer";

export function resolveTutorMode({
  userAnswer,
  answerRevealed,
}: {
  userAnswer: string | null | undefined;
  answerRevealed: boolean;
}): TutorMode {
  if (answerRevealed) return "post-answer";
  // Before an answer is committed, the Explanation button opens the structural
  // preview rather than streaming coaching.
  return userAnswer ? "socratic" : "preview";
}

export function resolveTutorLaunch({
  tutorModeEnabled,
  correct,
  answerRevealed,
}: {
  tutorModeEnabled: boolean;
  correct: boolean;
  answerRevealed: boolean;
}): { shouldOpen: boolean; mode: TutorMode } {
  return {
    shouldOpen: tutorModeEnabled && !correct,
    mode: resolveTutorMode({ userAnswer: "submitted", answerRevealed }),
  };
}

Mirrors LSAT Lab's Answer button (\"Show correct answer\" / hide).\n_Avoid_: reveal (the code's word; fine in code, but \"reveal\" sounds one-way and\nthis is a toggle), solution, show answer.\n\n**Explanation** (the pill):\nThe on/off control beside Answer that opens the whole Question Explanation:\nthe Argument Breakdown under the stimulus and in the drawer, and every Choice\nNote expanded. Off folds all of it away and leaves the question exactly as it\nwas, including any Answer colouring already on. Never opens on its own: a wrong\nsubmit does not press it, only the student does. Mirrors LSAT Lab's\nExplanation button (\"Hide explanations\").\n_Avoid_: breakdown (that is one half of what it opens), tutor, coach.\n\n**Argument Breakdown**:\nThe question-level half of a Question Explanation: the ordered labelled steps\n(Background, Evidence, Conclusion, Evaluate, Goal, and so on) plus the style\npills (Standard / Brief / Another Way) when the source published more than one\nstyle. Lives under the stimulus while Explanation is on, and is repeated in the\ndrawer's Explanation tab.\n_Avoid_: explanation (the pill), breakdown steps, analysis.\n\n**Choice Note**:\nThe per-choice half of a Question Explanation: one choice's tag (\"Out of\nScope\", \"Premise\"), its \"% of students\", and its prose. Rendered as a dropdown\nunder that choice, open by default while Explanation is on, with a chevron the\nstudent can fold and reopen per choice. A choice with nothing published gets\nno dropdown at all, never an empty one.\n_Avoid_: inline explanation (the component's old name), answer explanation,\nrationale.\n\n**Reset Response**:\nThe control under the choices that clears the student's pick and nothing else.\nThe timer keeps running, the Answer and Explanation pills keep their state, and\nan attempt already logged stays logged. Greyed out until a choice is picked.\nA pick made after a Reset on an already-attempted question is practice, not a\nsecond attempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab's Reset Response button.\n_Avoid_: undo, clear, retry (retry implies a new scored attempt).\n\n**AI Tutor**:\nThe generated coaching surfaces on the drill screen: the Coach chat that used to\nopen after a wrong answer, the \"Ask Joshua about this question\" hand-off on a\nquestion with no authored prose, and the \"Explain your reasoning\" voice chip.\nDistinct from the Question Explanation, which is transcribed LSAT Lab content\nand never generated. Off for every student as of 2026-09-12 behind one switch;\nthe code stays so it can be turned back on.\n_Avoid_: tutor mode (the code's flag name), Joshua (the chat's persona; a\nstudent reading \"Joshua is having trouble\" thinks the person is), coach (one of\nits tabs).\nEOF\ntail -3 src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md"}

/**
 * Lists which preptests actually have an LSAT Lab explanation sidecar.
 *
 * `mergeExplanationSidecars` used to derive its fetch list from the question
 * bank, so it asked for a sidecar for every PT the bank contains. The bank has
 * 59; 21 are authored. The other 38 requests 404 on every cold load of the
 * classroom. Nothing breaks — the loader treats a miss as "no explanations yet"
 * — but it is 38 wasted round-trips, and it fills the console with red on a
 * page where a real error would then be invisible.
 *
 * Unlike the finder address index, a glob over public/data IS the right source
 * of truth here: the sidecars are optional content, and a file's existence is
 * exactly the question being asked. A stray file can only add explanations to
 * questions that already exist, and the loader ignores ids it does not know.
 *
 *   node scripts/build-explanations-manifest.mjs
 */
import { readdirSync, writeFileSync, existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve, dirname } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const root = resolve(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
const dir = resolve(root, "public/data/explanations");
const out = resolve(dir, "index.json");

if (!existsSync(dir)) {
  console.error(`Explanations manifest: ${dir} does not exist; nothing to do.`);
  process.exit(0);
}

const pts = readdirSync(dir)
  .map((name) => /^PT(\d+)-explanations\.json$/.exec(name))
  .filter(Boolean)
  .map((m) => Number(m[1]))
  .sort((a, b) => a - b);

writeFileSync(out, JSON.stringify({ pts }, null, 2) + "\n", "utf8");
console.log(`Explanations manifest: ${pts.length} sidecar(s) -> ${out.slice(root.length + 1)}`);

---
1. **Blocker — Answer and Explanation remain coupled.** `Drill.tsx:3633` explicitly hides Answer when Explanation closes; opening Explanation calls the answer-reveal path. The query (`:2987`), drawer (`:3815`), and Choice Notes also require `currentRevealed`. Adding the breakdown slot does not fix independence.  
   **Fix:** Separate Explanation visibility and data loading from Answer colouring, update both toggle handlers, and test all four toggle combinations.

2. **Blocker — Reset cannot reach the proposed practice Submit.** Both official bars choose Next whenever `currentAttempted` is true (`Drill.tsx:4200`, `:4259`), and Reset deliberately preserves that attempt. Furthermore, the adaptive existing-attempt branch restores the original selection (`:1987`).  
   **Fix:** Add explicit per-question practice-response state that exposes practice Submit and never restores or rewrites the recorded answer.

3. **High — The duplicate guard can permanently suppress retry after a failed save.** `handleSubmitNonAdaptive` reports persistence failure but still inserts into `session.attempts` and updates adaptive statistics (`Drill.tsx:1744–1780`). The proposed `attempts.has(qid)` guard then treats that unsaved answer as committed.  
   **Fix:** Return failure before recording success, and distinguish pending/failed persistence from a successfully committed attempt.

4. **High — The one-line guard does not prevent concurrent submissions.** Nonadaptive Submit stays available while persistence awaits; two calls can both observe no attempt and write twice. Adaptive already has an in-flight guard (`Drill.tsx:2012`); the other path does not.  
   **Fix:** Apply a synchronous per-run/qid in-flight guard to every commit path and test double submission with delayed persistence.

5. **High — “A pick after an attempt is practice” is false in practice-set mode.** `handleAnswerSelect` immediately replaces or deletes `session.attempts` for practice sets (`Drill.tsx:1335–1375`); elimination can delete the entry too (`:1392`). Submit guards cannot preserve work already changed by selection.  
   **Fix:** Explicitly scope practice-response behaviour by mode and keep post-attempt picks separate from scored attempts in every included mode.

6. **High — The sealed-run selection instruction is dangerously ambiguous.** “A pick is refused only in a sealed run” reads as blocking every answer in a practice-test sitting or sealed section, although those runs currently accept and revise selections.  
   **Fix:** Specify the exact guard predicate and retain existing sealed-run selection, revision, and navigation behaviour with regression tests.

7. **High — Step 2 misses the live fixed-queue submit wrapper and the auto-reveal policy.** `Drill.tsx:4261–4283` contains another tutor launch and only colours answers when `autoReveal` is true; adaptive Submit also passes `autoReveal`. The promised unconditional wrong-answer colouring conflicts with that existing control.  
   **Fix:** Define whether wrong submissions override auto-reveal, implement that policy in both official submit paths, and update the control’s meaning accordingly.

8. **Medium — Initializing tutor state to false is not a master switch.** The overlay mounts based only on `tutorChatOpen` and its snapshot (`Drill.tsx:3649`); legacy controls can set tutor state or open chat directly (`:4417–4431`). Conversely, flipping the flag back on does not restore the official toggle, which is hardcoded invisible (`:4089`).  
   **Fix:** Gate launch handlers, toggle availability, and overlay mounting with the constant, independently of mutable tutor state.

9. **Medium — The “unchanged” empty-note rule contradicts the plan.** `isInlineChoiceEmpty` returns false for any selected choice, even without published content; whitespace-only notes also count as nonempty. Adding a chevron would create empty dropdowns.  
   **Fix:** Determine disclosure eligibility from trimmed published content, and render “Your answer” separately from an empty disclosure.

10. **Medium — The census and fallback overclaim what the data proves.** `fetchLrExplanation` returns null for an uningested question, while `hasAuthored` counts step/note entries without checking their prose. Neither result establishes that a video exists, yet the plan labels missing prose “video only.”  
    **Fix:** Share a trimmed-prose predicate and distinguish not ingested, no authored prose, and verified video-only using actual source evidence.

11. **Medium — Census completeness is unspecified.** The plan borrows environment loading from `db-pull.mjs` but omits its pagination safeguard; truncated table reads would falsely classify bank questions as missing.  
    **Fix:** Require deterministic pagination for every table, reconcile counts, and abort publication on query failure or incomplete retrieval.

12. **Medium — Verification misses the interactions most likely to break.** One Smart Drill screenshot does not exercise fixed queues, practice sets, sealed runs, failed saves, cold explanation loading, or the new scroll container at narrow widths. The repository also requires the full responsive matrix.  
    **Fix:** Add targeted integration checks for these cases and browser verification at narrow, mid, wide, and sidecar-docked sizes.

VERDICT: REVISE
